صَفًّا retains a day-event angel scene, secondary to 78:38."}],"channel_subchannels_anchored_here":[{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Measured Ascent and Collapsing Distance","parent_semantic_invariant":"A remote-looking event is recalibrated by a higher scale whose route, duration, and endpoint make it near.","parent_surprising_reach":"The scale is not only numerical; it becomes a route with bends, halts, visible heights, and an endpoint that closes distance.","key":"A","name":"Calibrated ascent","reading_type":"surface-primary","scene_or_process":"An ascent is assigned a day, a quantity, a five-based count, a thousand-fold magnitude, and a span of years.","active_motifs":"upward ascent `quranic:root_000997:B004/m01`; bounded amount `quranic:root_001205:B001/m01`; the number five `quranic:root_000439:B001/m01`; one thousand `quranic:root_000045:B001/m01`; year-long term `quranic:root_000751:B008/m01`; bounded daylight `quranic:root_001700:B001/m01`; indefinite temporal duration `quranic:root_001700:B002/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:3-4` مَعَارِج/تَعْرُج (`ع ر ج`); `70:4` يَوْم، مِقْدَارُهُ، خَمْسِينَ، أَلْفَ، سَنَة (`ي و م`, `ق د ر`, `خ م س`, `ء ل ف`, `س ن و`).","synthesis":"The ascent is rendered as a calibrated operation: a path rises while day, amount, five, thousand, and year supply nested units. The apparently excessive duration does not defer the endpoint; it identifies the scale on which the ascent is already measured.","ayah_refs":["70:3","70:4"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Measured Ascent and Collapsing Distance","parent_semantic_invariant":"A remote-looking event is recalibrated by a higher scale whose route, duration, and endpoint make it near.","parent_surprising_reach":"The scale is not only numerical; it becomes a route with bends, halts, visible heights, and an endpoint that closes distance.","key":"B","name":"Bent route to a visible height","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A route inclines, bends, pauses, and rises until it reaches an exposed high place.","active_motifs":"bent course `quranic:root_000997:B002/m01`; halt on a route `quranic:root_000997:B003/m01`; upward passage `quranic:root_000997:B004/m01`; elevation `quranic:root_000745:B001/m01`; distant visible height `quranic:root_000745:B002/m01`; overhead canopy `quranic:root_000745:B004/m01`; raised rank `quranic:root_000751:B003/m01`; flood-safe high ground `quranic:root_001476:B004/m01`; elevated cloud or ground `quranic:root_001675:B016/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:3-4` مَعَارِج/تَعْرُج (`ع ر ج`); `70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:8` السَّمَاء (`س م و`); `70:14` يُنْجِيه (`ن ج و`).","synthesis":"The lexical path is not a featureless vertical line. It has inclination, stopping points, and visible high ground, so ascent becomes a traversed geography. The high refuge motif also pressures the rescue wish in `70:14`: even terrain above a flood belongs to the same measured route.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:3","70:4","70:8","70:14"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] The Falling Event as Hydraulic Breach","parent_semantic_invariant":"What falls moves like a fluid surge through channels, pools, and barriers until defenses cease to regulate it.","parent_surprising_reach":"The punishment acquires the mechanics of rain, flood discharge, reservoirs, and failed waterworks.","key":"A","name":"Rainfall, discharge, and catchment","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Water falls, arrives in a single discharge, crowds into a surge, runs through outlets, and settles in catchments.","active_motifs":"falling rain `quranic:root_001675:B003/m01`; water-holding hollow `quranic:root_001675:B008/m01`; one burst of liquid `quranic:root_000480:B004/m01`; flood and wave surge `quranic:root_000480:B005/m01`; water channels and outfalls `quranic:root_000480:B006/m01`; flood barrier with measured release `quranic:root_000751:B007/m01`; mountain channel `quranic:root_001159:B006/m01`; waterskin `quranic:root_001212:B009/m01`; hot water and spring `quranic:root_000001:B002/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:7` قَرِيب (`ق ر ب`); `70:10` حَمِيم (`ح م م`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`).","synthesis":"Falling becomes a complete hydraulic sequence: precipitation, pulse, surge, channel, and basin. The hot-water motif converts an ordinary catchment into a punitive reservoir, while the mountain channel anticipates terrain that can no longer hold its form.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:7","70:10","70:13"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] The Falling Event as Hydraulic Breach","parent_semantic_invariant":"What falls moves like a fluid surge through channels, pools, and barriers until defenses cease to regulate it.","parent_surprising_reach":"The punishment acquires the mechanics of rain, flood discharge, reservoirs, and failed waterworks.","key":"B","name":"Barrier and repeller failure","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Protective works try to divert, delay, wall off, or stopper an arriving force, but the event is fixed and severe.","active_motifs":"diverting a harmful force `quranic:root_000480:B001/m01`; delaying a claim `quranic:root_000480:B008/m01`; controlled flood barrier `quranic:root_000751:B007/m01`; stopper for a vessel or well `quranic:root_000840:B018/m01`; covered mountain pass or low wall `quranic:root_001307:B013/m01`; short defensive wall `quranic:root_001159:B007/m01`; fixed falling occurrence `quranic:root_001675:B001/m01`; severe descending event `quranic:root_001675:B002/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:5` اصْبِر (`ص ب ر`); `70:2` كَافِرِين (`ك ف ر`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`).","synthesis":"Diversion, postponement, gate, stopper, pass, and wall are successive control mechanisms. Their assembly sharpens “no repeller”: the failure is not lack of one heroic defender but the exhaustion of every ordinary means by which a flow is checked.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:5","70:13"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] The Falling Event as Hydraulic Breach","parent_semantic_invariant":"What falls moves like a fluid surge through channels, pools, and barriers until defenses cease to regulate it.","parent_surprising_reach":"The punishment acquires the mechanics of rain, flood discharge, reservoirs, and failed waterworks.","key":"C","name":"Watering interval, thirst, and pre-birth flow","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A herd approaches water on a counted cycle while thirst, irrigation, milk, and visible pregnancy regulate access to life-giving fluid.","active_motifs":"five-day camel watering cycle `quranic:root_000439:B003/m01`; alternating camel-water schedule `quranic:root_000997:B006/m01`; sweet potable water `quranic:root_000994:B001/m01`; abstention from food and drink `quranic:root_000994:B002/m01`; irrigation by drawing water `quranic:root_000751:B001/m01`; night approach to a water source `quranic:root_001212:B008/m01`; milk entering the udder before birth `quranic:root_000480:B009/m01`; visible animal pregnancy `quranic:root_000531:B010/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1,11` عَذَاب (`ع ذ ب`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:3-4` مَعَارِج/تَعْرُج (`ع ر ج`); `70:4` خَمْسِين، سَنَة (`خ م س`, `س ن و`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى، قَرِيب (`ر ء ي`, `ق ر ب`).","synthesis":"Counted time becomes a husbandry schedule rather than an abstraction. A thirsty herd, drawn irrigation, approach to the source, and milk arriving before birth form a life-maintaining cycle that the fifty-thousand-year measure radically enlarges.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:3","70:4","70:6"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Thermal Unmaking of Sky, Mountain, and Body","parent_semantic_invariant":"Heat converts stable forms into melt, fiber, cooked flesh, and stripped residue.","parent_surprising_reach":"Cosmic collapse is rendered through kitchen, foundry, textile, and anatomical operations.","key":"A","name":"Molten canopy and rendered fat","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"The overhead canopy becomes a molten compound while fat, grease, and metal lose their former boundaries.","active_motifs":"molten metal, oil dregs, and pitch-like liquid `quranic:root_001452:B003/m01`; melted fat `quranic:root_000001:B003/m01`; rendered grease `quranic:root_000260:B005/m01`; rump fat and flesh `quranic:root_000076:B015/m01`; overhead sky or canopy `quranic:root_000745:B004/m01`; occurrence and transformation in time `quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:4` كَان (`ك و ن`); `70:8` تَكُون، السَّمَاء، الْمُهْل (`ك و ن`, `س م و`, `م ه ل`); `70:5` جَمِيلًا (`ج م ل`); `70:10` حَمِيم (`ح م م`); surface anchor unavailable for `ء ل ي` at `70:4`.","synthesis":"The molten comparison opens into rendering and smelting: canopy, metal, oil sediment, and animal fat enter one phase change. The sky is not merely colored like melt; it behaves as matter whose internal distinctions have been cooked away.","ayah_refs":["70:4","70:8","70:5","70:10"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Kinship Converted into Ransom Inventory","parent_semantic_invariant":"Relations that ordinarily shelter, love, and remain near are recoded as substitutable assets offered for one person's release.","parent_surprising_reach":"The family list becomes at once a household map, a surety system, a prisoner exchange, and a total inventory.","key":"B","name":"Substitution, surety, and prisoner exchange","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A liable person tries to place another life, property, or guarantor between himself and the penalty.","active_motifs":"sacrificial substitute `quranic:root_001136:B001/m01`; prisoner exchange `quranic:root_001136:B002/m01`; using another as protective ransom `quranic:root_001136:B003/m01`; diverting harm `quranic:root_000480:B001/m01`; ownership and disposal `quranic:root_001444:B002/m01`; effective capacity `quranic:root_001205:B003/m01`; surety and personal guarantee `quranic:root_000840:B003/m01`; undertaking care for another `quranic:root_001332:B003/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` مِقْدَار (`ق د ر`), مَلَائِكَة (`م ل ك`), كَان (`ك و ن`); `70:5` اصْبِر (`ص ب ر`); `70:11` يَفْتَدِي (`ف د ي`).","synthesis":"Ransom is a juridical mechanism rather than a vague wish: it requires a substitute, transferable control, capacity to tender it, and a surety accepted by the other side. “No repeller” means this proposed exchange has no receiving institution and no valid guarantor.","ayah_refs":["70:2","70:4","70:5","70:11"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Kinship Converted into Ransom Inventory","parent_semantic_invariant":"Relations that ordinarily shelter, love, and remain near are recoded as substitutable assets offered for one person's release.","parent_surprising_reach":"The family list becomes at once a household map, a surety system, a prisoner exchange, and a total inventory.","key":"C","name":"Everyone and everything gathered as one lot","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Distributed persons and goods are compressed into a complete, undivided offer.","active_motifs":"ground and all that is below the sky `quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`; thick woolen ground-covering `quranic:root_000025:B005/m01`; gathering dispersed things `quranic:root_000259:B001/m01`; assembled people or mixed host `quranic:root_000259:B002/m01`; undiminished totality `quranic:root_000259:B009/m01`; immense full container `quranic:root_000259:B012/m01`; comprehensive total `quranic:root_000260:B003/m01`; joining separated parts `quranic:root_000045:B002/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:4` أَلْف (`ء ل ف`); `70:5` جَمِيلًا (`ج م ل`); `70:14` الْأَرْض جَمِيعًا (`ء ر ض`, `ج م ع`); `70:18` جَمَع (`ج م ع`).","synthesis":"“Everyone on earth” is lexically materialized as mixed host, complete body, full vessel, and joined total. The proposed ransom thus grows from family substitutions into a single lot containing the inhabited world.","ayah_refs":["70:4","70:5","70:14","70:18"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Visibility, Bodily Evidence, and Inscribed Judgment","parent_semantic_invariant":"What was estimated, hidden, or relationally ignored is made perceptible through sight, marks, seams, blood, and text.","parent_surprising_reach":"Visual disclosure extends into mirror, banner, blood trace, stitched edge, textual division, and signature.","key":"B","name":"Mark, division, and written closure","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A matter is made legible by banner, articulated speech, textual segmentation, detail, and a final written mark.","active_motifs":"visible standard `quranic:root_000531:B011/m01`; tongue that distinguishes matters `quranic:root_001159:B004/m01`; textual and metrical divisions `quranic:root_001159:B011/m01`; analytic detailing `quranic:root_001159:B013/m01`; grammatical separator `quranic:root_001159:B015/m01`; signature or marginal notation `quranic:root_001675:B011/m01`; projected voice `quranic:root_000239:B008/m01`; alphabetic letter `quranic:root_000045:B006/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:4` أَلْف (`ء ل ف`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى (`ر ء ي`); `70:11` الْمُجْرِم (`ج ر م`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`).","synthesis":"Judgment becomes an act of legibility. A standard points, a tongue distinguishes, divisions organize, detail discloses components, and a signature closes the record. The relatives listed in discrete sequence read like the articulated sections of the criminal's liability.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:4","70:6","70:11","70:13"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Reversal, Pursuit, and the End of the Remnant","parent_semantic_invariant":"Turning one's back initiates a directional sequence in which the rejected call becomes the pursuing endpoint.","parent_surprising_reach":"Front/back orientation expands into route succession, chase, failed escape, and eradication of the last trace.","key":"B","name":"Movement converging on a terminal point","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Rapid motion, succession, and pursuit converge on a limit that is reached and possessed.","active_motifs":"forceful movement toward a goal `quranic:root_000480:B003/m01`; released running `quranic:root_001489:B012/m01`; uninterrupted succession `quranic:root_001684:B002/m01`; reaching and taking the goal `quranic:root_001684:B012/m01`; terminal limit `quranic:root_000076:B001/m01`; vocal attraction toward a place `quranic:root_000478:B001/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:16` نَزَّاعَة (`ن ز ع`); `70:17` تَدْعُو، تَوَلَّى (`د ع و`, `و ل ي`); surface anchor unavailable for `ء ل ي` in `70:4`.","synthesis":"Retreat does not open indefinite distance. Motion follows motion without a gap, the endpoint is reached, and the call functions like a directional force. The terminal preposition motif makes the pursuit grammatically as well as physically convergent.","ayah_refs":["70:2","70:16","70:17","70:4"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Reversal, Pursuit, and the End of the Remnant","parent_semantic_invariant":"Turning one's back initiates a directional sequence in which the rejected call becomes the pursuing endpoint.","parent_surprising_reach":"Front/back orientation expands into route succession, chase, failed escape, and eradication of the last trace.","key":"C","name":"Cutting off the last trace","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Death, banishment, and extraction proceed until no successor or remnant remains.","active_motifs":"eradication of a people's last trace `quranic:root_000458:B004/m01`; surviving scrap `quranic:root_000830:B004/m01`; banishment into destruction `quranic:root_000131:B004/m01`; death as release `quranic:root_000609:B016/m01`; decreed death `quranic:root_000001:B010/m01`; terminal death-pull `quranic:root_001489:B016/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:4` الرُّوح (`ر و ح`); `70:6` بَعِيدًا (`ب ع د`); `70:10` حَمِيم (`ح م م`); `70:16` نَزَّاعَة، الشَّوَى (`ن ز ع`, `ش و ي`); `70:17` أَدْبَر (`د ب ر`).","synthesis":"The active contrast is between a leftover piece and the cutting of the last trace. Death is rendered as decree, release, and final extraction, so the pursuing call ends not merely in capture but in removal of continuity.","ayah_refs":["70:4","70:6","70:10","70:16","70:17"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Hoard as Vessel, Fist, and Shackle","parent_semantic_invariant":"Accumulation compresses dispersed value into a controlled interior, but the same enclosing operation converts possession into restraint.","parent_surprising_reach":"The hoard takes the forms of fist, full vessel, rope, stopper, healed fracture, and hand-to-neck iron.","key":"A","name":"Compressing a dispersed stock","reading_type":"surface-primary","scene_or_process":"Goods from separate places are gathered, gripped, completed, and put into a container.","active_motifs":"gathering dispersed property `quranic:root_000259:B001/m01`; closed fist and handful `quranic:root_000259:B005/m01`; intact undiminished whole `quranic:root_000259:B009/m01`; force or flow gathering itself `quranic:root_000259:B010/m01`; great full vessel `quranic:root_000259:B012/m01`; enclosing goods in a vessel `quranic:root_001664:B002/m01`; many persons collected together `quranic:root_001664:B008/m01`; comprehensive sum `quranic:root_000260:B003/m01`; joining separated pieces `quranic:root_000045:B002/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:4` أَلْف (`ء ل ف`); `70:5` جَمِيلًا (`ج م ل`); `70:14,18` جَمِيعًا/جَمَع (`ج م ع`); `70:18` أَوْعَى (`و ع ي`).","synthesis":"Gathering proceeds through increasingly compact states: dispersed stock, collective mass, complete whole, handful, and sealed vessel. The hoard is therefore a spatial operation of reducing plurality to an interior under one holder's control.","ayah_refs":["70:4","70:5","70:14","70:18"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Hoard as Vessel, Fist, and Shackle","parent_semantic_invariant":"Accumulation compresses dispersed value into a controlled interior, but the same enclosing operation converts possession into restraint.","parent_surprising_reach":"The hoard takes the forms of fist, full vessel, rope, stopper, healed fracture, and hand-to-neck iron.","key":"C","name":"Wealth, collusion, and obsessive possession","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Property is acquired, guarded by alliance, and pursued until engagement with it becomes compulsive.","active_motifs":"enduring large estate `quranic:root_000458:B016/m01`; ownership and disposal `quranic:root_001444:B002/m01`; collusive assembly around an affair `quranic:root_000259:B013/m01`; obsessive immersion in a matter `quranic:root_000480:B011/m01`; gain and acquisition `quranic:root_000239:B003/m01`; gathered food-stock or marketable dates `quranic:root_001136:B004/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` مَلَائِكَة (`م ل ك`); `70:11` الْمُجْرِم، يَفْتَدِي (`ج ر م`, `ف د ي`); `70:14,18` جَمِيعًا/جَمَع (`ج م ع`); `70:17` أَدْبَر (`د ب ر`).","synthesis":"The hoard is sustained socially as well as materially: acquisition feeds an estate, allies coordinate around it, and the possessor becomes engrossed in its defense. That network explains why accumulation can compete with every prior kinship claim.","ayah_refs":["70:2","70:4","70:11","70:14","70:17"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Caravan Order and the Wounded Mount","parent_semantic_invariant":"A herd survives by ordered leadership, water, route, and tack, yet the same journey leaves the bearing animal abraded and spent.","parent_surprising_reach":"The procession includes a herd leader, valley centerline, water reserve, honored mount, saddle underlay, and paired riding wounds.","key":"A","name":"Herd, leader, and return","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A collected herd follows a leading animal and returns toward its managed resting place.","active_motifs":"camel herd with keepers `quranic:root_000260:B001/m01`; large camel group `quranic:root_000997:B005/m01`; leading animal followed by the rest `quranic:root_001444:B008/m01`; livestock returning in the afternoon `quranic:root_000609:B005/m01`; animals kneeling to rest `quranic:root_001675:B007/m01`; capable management of livestock wealth `quranic:root_001056:B007/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:3-4` مَعَارِج/تَعْرُج (`ع ر ج`); `70:4` الرُّوح، مَلَائِكَة (`ر و ح`, `م ل ك`); `70:5` جَمِيلًا (`ج م ل`); `70:9` الْعِهْن (`ع ه ن`).","synthesis":"The ascent is reframed as a managed procession: herd, leader, keepers, timed return, and kneeling place. Order depends on following the guide; turning away from the ordered line in `70:17` is therefore a pastoral as well as moral dislocation.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:3","70:4","70:5","70:9"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Caravan Order and the Wounded Mount","parent_semantic_invariant":"A herd survives by ordered leadership, water, route, and tack, yet the same journey leaves the bearing animal abraded and spent.","parent_surprising_reach":"The procession includes a herd leader, valley centerline, water reserve, honored mount, saddle underlay, and paired riding wounds.","key":"B","name":"Route, tack, and abrasion","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A mount follows the center of a route with water enough for travel, protected by tack that nonetheless produces sores.","active_motifs":"ship-rope or heavy lead cord `quranic:root_000260:B002/m01`; saddle underlay `quranic:root_001684:B011/m01`; saddle sore `quranic:root_000458:B017/m01`; riding abrasion `quranic:root_001675:B010/m01`; centerline of road or valley `quranic:root_001444:B006/m01`; water that sustains a traveler `quranic:root_001444:B007/m01`; prepared honored mount `quranic:root_001212:B013/m01`; camel exempted from burden `quranic:root_000480:B010/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` مَلَائِكَة (`م ل ك`); `70:5` جَمِيلًا (`ج م ل`); `70:7` قَرِيب (`ق ر ب`); `70:17` أَدْبَر، تَوَلَّى (`د ب ر`, `و ل ي`).","synthesis":"Route and tack distribute a journey's load: centerline guides, water sustains, underlay cushions, and an honored mount is spared work. The paired riding sores expose the failure of that distribution under extreme duration: what carries the procession is itself marked by the passage.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:5","70:7","70:17"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Gestation, Weaning, and the Severed Offspring Bond","parent_semantic_invariant":"Life is joined through mating and gestation, then differentiated through birth, weaning, and maturation.","parent_surprising_reach":"The family list expands into animal breeding, visible pregnancy, uterine tissue, milk-before-birth, weaning, and the adult child becoming a companion.","key":"A","name":"Mating, pregnancy, and pre-birth provision","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A male mounts, pregnancy becomes visible, birth approaches, and milk enters the udder in advance.","active_motifs":"stallion mounting a herd `quranic:root_000745:B003/m01`; male camel covering the female `quranic:root_000751:B010/m01`; horse becoming breeding male `quranic:root_000609:B018/m01`; visible animal pregnancy `quranic:root_000531:B010/m01`; imminent birth `quranic:root_001212:B012/m01`; milk before parturition `quranic:root_000480:B009/m01`; uterine vessels `quranic:root_001056:B005/m01`; womb and post-birth discharge `quranic:root_000994:B009/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1,11` عَذَاب (`ع ذ ب`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` الرُّوح، سَنَة (`ر و ح`, `س ن و`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى، قَرِيب (`ر ء ي`, `ق ر ب`); `70:8` السَّمَاء (`س م و`); `70:9` الْعِهْن (`ع ه ن`).","synthesis":"Reproduction is a causal chain rather than a family label: mating, conception, visible swelling, approaching birth, uterine structure, and anticipatory milk. The son's presence in `70:11` carries this whole material history, intensifying the violence of offering him as an exchange object.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:6","70:8","70:9"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Cultivation, Cutting, and the Hoarded Harvest","parent_semantic_invariant":"Rain and soil produce covered growth that is cut, sorted, and gathered into post-harvest stock.","parent_surprising_reach":"The hoard resembles a crop economy whose seed-covering, irrigation, palm propagation, cutting, gleaning, drying, and piling have been detached from redistribution.","key":"A","name":"Soil, rain, and emerging growth","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Rain reaches fertile ground, plants root and unfold, while dry growth marks the opposite state.","active_motifs":"fertile planted earth `quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`; rainfall `quranic:root_001675:B003/m01`; leaf and aromatic plant `quranic:root_000609:B012/m01`; tree bursting into leaf `quranic:root_000609:B017/m01`; named herb `quranic:root_001489:B015/m01`; dry tree `quranic:root_000217:B008/m01`; green tanning shrub `quranic:root_000076:B005/m01`; medicinal senna plant `quranic:root_000751:B005/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:4` الرُّوح، سَنَة (`ر و ح`, `س ن و`); `70:9` الْجِبَال (`ج ب ل`); `70:14` الْأَرْض (`ء ر ض`); `70:16` نَزَّاعَة (`ن ز ع`); surface anchor unavailable for `ء ل ي` at `70:4`.","synthesis":"The earth is not inert inventory. It receives rain, holds rooted growth, opens leaves, and yields named plants; drought produces the dry-tree counterstate. Offering “all on earth” would therefore consume a productive ecology, not merely a territorial expanse.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:4","70:9","70:14","70:16"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Cultivation, Cutting, and the Hoarded Harvest","parent_semantic_invariant":"Rain and soil produce covered growth that is cut, sorted, and gathered into post-harvest stock.","parent_surprising_reach":"The hoard resembles a crop economy whose seed-covering, irrigation, palm propagation, cutting, gleaning, drying, and piling have been detached from redistribution.","key":"B","name":"Covering seed and governing water","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Seed is covered, fruit remains sheathed, and irrigation is admitted through regulated barriers into protected catchments.","active_motifs":"sowing by covering seed `quranic:root_001307:B008/m01`; fruit sheath `quranic:root_001307:B010/m01`; drawing irrigation water `quranic:root_000751:B001/m01`; flood-control barrier `quranic:root_000751:B007/m01`; water-holding ground `quranic:root_001675:B008/m01`; raised ground above flood `quranic:root_001476:B004/m01`; mountain watercourse `quranic:root_001159:B006/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` كَافِرِين (`ك ف ر`); `70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`); `70:14` يُنْجِيه (`ن ج و`).","synthesis":"Productive enclosure differs from hoarding: soil covers seed to germinate, a sheath protects fruit, and a barrier releases only needed water. These regulated containers expose the moral defect of accumulation in `70:18`, which closes without a life-giving outlet.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:13","70:14"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Cultivation, Cutting, and the Hoarded Harvest","parent_semantic_invariant":"Rain and soil produce covered growth that is cut, sorted, and gathered into post-harvest stock.","parent_surprising_reach":"The hoard resembles a crop economy whose seed-covering, irrigation, palm propagation, cutting, gleaning, drying, and piling have been detached from redistribution.","key":"C","name":"Cutting, gleaning, and piling","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Mature growth is cut, residual dates are gleaned, palm stock is propagated, and food is amassed into an unmeasured pile.","active_motifs":"cutting fruit and shearing `quranic:root_000239:B001/m01`; dates left after harvest `quranic:root_000239:B002/m01`; seed-grown date palms of mixed kind `quranic:root_000259:B011/m01`; transplanted palm shoot `quranic:root_001159:B016/m01`; piled food sold without measure `quranic:root_000840:B011/m01`; gathered dates or grain `quranic:root_001136:B004/m01`; taking the whole stock `quranic:root_000751:B009/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:5` صَبْر (`ص ب ر`); `70:11` الْمُجْرِم، يَفْتَدِي (`ج ر م`, `ف د ي`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`); `70:14,18` جَمِيعًا/جَمَع (`ج م ع`).","synthesis":"Harvest separates crop from plant, then recovers leftovers and gathers them into a pile. The final “taking the whole stock” motif turns gleaning's residual economy into total appropriation, aligning the harvest scene tightly with the hoarder.","ayah_refs":["70:4","70:5","70:11","70:13","70:14"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Animated Body, Suppurating Wound, and Death-Pull","parent_semantic_invariant":"The living body is an assembled form sustained by breath, then marked by heat and injury before animation is withdrawn.","parent_surprising_reach":"Breath, lung, bodily frame, fever, sweat, saddle sore, pus, and death-throe form a complete deterioration sequence.","key":"A","name":"Breath within an assembled frame","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Life inhabits a structured body whose lungs, torso, and extremities remain perceptible.","active_motifs":"life-giving spirit `quranic:root_000609:B001/m01`; lung and its ailments `quranic:root_000531:B009/m01`; bodily mass `quranic:root_000239:B007/m01`; composed bodily structure `quranic:root_000156:B002/m01`; extremities and scalp `quranic:root_000830:B002/m01`; rump flesh `quranic:root_000076:B015/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:4` الرُّوح (`ر و ح`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى (`ر ء ي`); `70:11` الْمُجْرِم، بَنِيه (`ج ر م`, `ب ن ي`); `70:16` الشَّوَى (`ش و ي`); surface anchor unavailable for `ء ل ي` in `70:4`.","synthesis":"The body appears as a composed frame animated by spirit and breathing through lung. Naming the extremities against that whole makes stripping a disassembly of a living architecture, not a generic image of pain.","ayah_refs":["70:4","70:6","70:11","70:16"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Animated Body, Suppurating Wound, and Death-Pull","parent_semantic_invariant":"The living body is an assembled form sustained by breath, then marked by heat and injury before animation is withdrawn.","parent_surprising_reach":"Breath, lung, bodily frame, fever, sweat, saddle sore, pus, and death-throe form a complete deterioration sequence.","key":"C","name":"Animation withdrawn","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"The body reaches an inescapable terminal state in which life is released and pulled out.","active_motifs":"death named as release `quranic:root_000609:B016/m01`; decreed mortality `quranic:root_000001:B010/m01`; terminal death-pull `quranic:root_001489:B016/m01`; captive fixed for killing `quranic:root_000840:B002/m01`; escape by separation `quranic:root_001476:B001/m01`; severe ordeal with no outlet `quranic:root_000840:B006/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:4` الرُّوح (`ر و ح`); `70:5` صَبْر (`ص ب ر`); `70:10` حَمِيم (`ح م م`); `70:14` يُنْجِيه (`ن ج و`); `70:16` نَزَّاعَة (`ن ز ع`).","synthesis":"Release is double-edged: rescue would separate a person from danger, while death releases spirit from body. The no-outlet ordeal collapses those alternatives, leaving extraction of life as the only completed separation.","ayah_refs":["70:4","70:5","70:10","70:14","70:16"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Sovereign Capacity, Crime, and Unaccepted Surety","parent_semantic_invariant":"A fixed penalty issues from effective dominion, distinguishes liability, and cannot be neutralized by private ownership or guarantee.","parent_surprising_reach":"Divine title, royal rule, calibrated design, adjudication, criminal gain, surety, and retribution form one legal order.","key":"A","name":"Dominion able to execute a measured decree","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A sovereign owns, measures, plans, and brings an event into occurrence.","active_motifs":"worshipped deity `quranic:root_000047:B001/m01`; divine name in invocation and oath `quranic:root_000047:B002/m01`; kingship and dominion `quranic:root_001444:B003/m01`; effective capacity `quranic:root_001205:B003/m01`; deliberate calibration `quranic:root_001205:B005/m01`; event occurring in time `quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`; governance and charge `quranic:root_001684:B003/m01`; entitlement and priority `quranic:root_001684:B008/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:3` اللَّه (`ء ل ه`); `70:4` مَلَائِكَة، كَان، مِقْدَار (`م ل ك`, `ك و ن`, `ق د ر`); `70:17` تَوَلَّى (`و ل ي`).","synthesis":"The source of punishment is specified through the operational vocabulary of rule: deity, dominion, capacity, measurement, planning, occurrence, and charge. Write in tr. Produce your response as three parts, in this order. If you return one combined response, label the parts clearly. If an orchestrator asks you to write the parts into separate files, omit wrapper labels from the prose and evidence files; the file path already supplies the label.

2. **The evidence surface** — separate from the prose, addressable per phrase, per `PRINCIPLES.md` §12.

## V2 Rendering Profile — 5.6 Sol High

This run tests whether `5.6-sol-high` can increase lexical depth while preserving
reader-facing clarity.

Increase depth through semantic precision, not additional bulk.

Before drafting, silently build a coverage ledger:

- include every `must_integrate` topic;
- include every `candidate` with a reader payoff not already expressed;
- if `v12_reader_responses` is absent because the default workflow retired
  per-ayah focus runs, do not infer a `stage_00` isolated response or
  confidence movement;
- if `v12_focus_trace_hermetic` is present, use its `baseline_models`,
  `context_deltas`, and `surprising_valid_outliers` as reconstructed
  before/after evidence. Do not call it a staged reveal transcript. Preserve
  surprising outliers when they remain anchored in this ayah, especially
  secondary split-root activations;
- include a regular reader-walk, plus/minus-5 reader-walk, whole-surah reading,
  or cross-run-publication item only when it adds a distinct retrospective
  insight or coverage check;
- use branch inventories to support explanations; they create no standalone
  prose obligation. When fallback inventories are present, treat them as
  restricted to this ayah's roots and anchored citations, not as a whole-surah
  obligation.
- treat `root_lexicon` as full dictionary/gloss support, not branch-filtered.
  It uses QAC-to-Furuq root mapping; when a QAC root is split, non-dominant
  Furuq targets are included as additional root entries and recorded in
  coverage. If multiple QAC roots map to the same Furuq root, the shared entry
  records all source roots in `qac_roots_ar` / `qac_root_mappings`; use those
  fields for evidence attribution when present, not the legacy single `root_ar`
  alone. Dictionary/gloss branches are evidence support, not independent
  obligations, unless tied to this ayah's word, topic, reader payoff, or cited
  relation. Full field means all distinct reader payoffs from activated
  material, not every dictionary branch for the root.
- if `channel_generated_outputs` lists quran-data files and this run gives you
  file access, read only those listed files when channel-family/path detail is
  necessary. Treat them as candidate/family/path evidence, not as an adjudicated
  channel ledger. State B channel restrictions still apply.

For each critical word, preserve these distinct layers when available:

1. local grammatical work;
2. locally selected sense;
3. coherent pressure supplied by activated or cited root branches;
4. one form, sound, rarity, or variant observation with a unique payoff;
5. later contextual change, compressed to its final reader-visible result.

Do not flatten these layers into a general metaphor. Deepen generic summaries by
naming the exact lexical mechanism. Ordering grammar and local meaning first is
grounding, not truth-ranking.

At first mention of an ayah word, use a structured Arabic surface span:
`{ar:surface_form, tr:Turkish-readable transliteration, gloss:target-language meaning}`.
Use the same full span again when the prose returns to that word after moving to
another word or another paragraph. Inside one short local sequence, after a full
span has just been given, a Turkish label or transliteration is enough. Explain
every technical term in the same sentence. Keep raw roots, root skeletons,
letter-by-letter root transliterations, and branch IDs in the evidence surface.
In prose, attach root discussion to the surface word:
`{ar:ٱلْعَادِيَاتِ, tr:el-âdiyât, gloss:koşup atılanlar} kelimesinin bağlı
olduğu kök alanı...`, not `ʿ-d-v kökü...`.

A paragraph may begin with the ayah's surface, a concrete image, or a
reader-facing claim. It may not begin with a bare grammar label or stacked
abstractions. Prefer "Âyet önce hamdi Allah'a verir; sabit ad cümlesi bu hamdi
yerleşik bir hüküm olarak taşır" over "Bu âyet, tek bir isim cümlesiyle yerleşik
bir hüküm kurar."

For this short ayah, keep at least two-thirds of the prose on its own wording.
Compress all later developments into at most three paragraphs and end with one
plain synthesis paragraph.

Use `v12_cross_run_publication`, if present, only as a compact coverage/priority
check derived from regular and plus/minus-5 reader runs. Do not copy it as prose,
and do not let it override local bundle evidence.

Use an explicit negative predicate only to correct a likely misconception,
protect the primary sense from replacement, or preserve live counter-evidence.
Default ceiling for this ayah: three. Never stack two negatives in one sentence.
During final revision, rewrite all other negatives as positive predication.
Do not drive negation to zero by default: if no explicit negative is needed,
include a profile-specific style audit in friction confirming that there was no
live misconception, replacement risk, or counter-evidence requiring one.

Pass only if every paragraph has one distinct reader payoff and the prose remains
clear with the evidence surface closed.
